mr. president, in many ways, a new era begins today. i would like to congratulate president tajani on his election. it is deeply moving that on this day, a day of celebration for him, his heart is in his homeland, italy, and he feels with those who suffered from today's earthquake. yesterday's presidential election had another clear winner in the person of our colleague, helga stevens, who was also a candidate and who, in her impassioned speech, used a slogan which i would like to bring to the attention of the maltese presidency, suggesting that they should have something similar as their slogan. mrs. stevens said that i may have been born deaf, but i have a very good ability to hear people's voices. i ask you to take on the mission for the next six months to teach european politicians to finally hear the voice of the european people. for example, in areas like immigration. hear the real stories of real people in europe, the so-called indigenous people, whose lives have been completely upended by mass immigration, especially women, whose lives have been shaped by a well-founded fear of crime. listen to these stories and make them part of the shaping or forming of your immigration policy. and let us also hear the voices of the so-called new member states, some of whom you are, for example, hungarian people are tired of the fact that we have been in the european union for more than ten years and that there are still four or five times the income differences between, say, hungary and austria. please address this in a social inclusion way. finally, accept the former socialist countries as equal members of the european union, and have the hungarian people equal in dignity with western people, and have similar income conditions, similar living standards.
mr president, as we have heard here on several occasions today, the biggest challenge for the maltese presidency will be to deal with the issue of migration and to strengthen internal security. of course, we must also emphasize, as has been said here many times today, that it would be good if he did not cast a shadow on the presidency, and that it would be appropriate to deal with the panama affair and the corruption cases that have arisen in connection with it, and to settle the matter as soon as possible. in the context of priorities, it is welcome that the presidency has set as its objective the stabilisation of our neighbourhood, in particular with regard to libya, and that it intends to tackle the root causes of migration and to eradicate human trafficking. at the same time, the objective must be to regain full control of the external borders. we must not allow another uncontrolled influx of people into europe. it is necessary to clarify that there is a legal order for entry into europe and to distinguish strictly between genuine protection-seekers and economic migrants. in parallel with the full protection of the external borders, safe zones outside the eu should be established. it should be possible for those points to process asylum applications outside the union.
in writing. i have said on several occasions in the past that the eu needs a more effective enlargement policy if it is to maintain its influence in the candidate countries and the trust of the european citizens living there. we cannot allow euroscepticism to divert the candidate countries from the path they have chosen, and the responsibility lies with national governments as well as the eu institutions. the government of hungary, in cooperation with the other visegrad countries, consistently supports the integration of the western balkans. good political and economic cooperation has been established with the countries of the region and hungary is ready to share with them the experience gained in the course of integration processes. i would like to thank the slovak presidency for making enlargement in the western balkans a priority and for working actively on it. in this light, it is legitimate to expect that the maltese presidency will look to them as a good example and that strengthening accession negotiations and opening further negotiating chapters will be high on their political agenda. the maltese presidency spokesman expressed confidence that they were committed to enlargement and would continue the negotiating process with serbia where the slovak presidency had left off.
in writing. the maltese presidency takes over the rotating presidency with honourable ambition at a time of extraordinary challenges for the future of the union as a whole. however, while agreeing with the priority package, it should be noted that at the end of the six-month period the assessment of the maltese presidency, its effectiveness, will be determined by the success of the process of creating unity among the member states and, on this basis, achieving concrete, legally binding eu action in dealing with the refugee crisis. i believe that this is also essential to take concrete measures to relocate refugees already in europe, to take urgent decisions to modernise (and save) the schengen system, and to promote a renewed eu migration policy that is much more compatible with efficiency and humanity than ever before. the council is also highly expected to do so because the member states most responsible for the inhumane treatment of refugees and the undermining of eu migration policy, and in particular the orbán government, are keen to blame brussels and the commission for the migration crisis, while seeking support from other member states. the maltese presidency will also be crucial in ensuring that a substantial number of member states, under the auspices of the presidency, act in a united and decisive manner against member states whose migration policies are contrary to the union's principles and interests.
on behalf of the s&d group. mr president, migration remains one of the most important problems in europe, in the european union. i am therefore very pleased to note that the council attaches great importance to the establishment of the european asylum agency and to the provision of the necessary financial resources. i am convinced that the other pillar of the common european asylum system, besides the border agency, should be the asylum agency. its main task is to support the offices and institutions of countries subject to significant migratory pressure and to provide humanitarian assistance and information to refugees. i think that mutual solidarity is the basis of everything, and those member states which are not willing to show solidarity with one another must certainly expect sanctions. i believe that the next step in the establishment of the asylum agency should be the start of the trilogue, and i, as rapporteur for the report, would like to assure both the commission and the council that we are ready to start constructive negotiations as soon as possible.
madam president, if we compare and review the conclusions of the european summits in october and december, there has been no progress in the main areas. on the issue of migration, both documents promise that the european council will decide how to apply the principle of solidarity in the future. we know that this means a mandatory admission quota, which is rejected by many countries in europe. nevertheless, it does state that existing resettlement programs must be accelerated. jean-claude juncker said here today that we must distinguish between terrorists and migrants. of course, we should, especially if we can, but we've heard that real political refugees should be distinguished from migrants. so, you're saying that's changed now? now the terrorist is the bad immigrant, all the rest are obliged to take? and we need safe routes for this, and we don't care about the safety of the population in western european cities?
in writing. the european council meeting of 15 december 2016 brought closer positions on the protection of the union's external borders, but further efforts are needed to coordinate migration policies. proper control of external borders is essential to reduce the pressure of immigration and to guarantee the security of citizens. the council paid particular attention to security, since the primary responsibility of european governments is to safeguard the security of their citizens. the uncontrolled flow of migrants into europe is unacceptable and unsustainable, and it is a threat to our security and to the democratic rule of law. the causes of illegal immigration must be addressed in the countries of origin, not just the consequences in the countries of destination in europe. the council reiterated its call for the acceleration of the relocation of immigrants, the mandatory resettlement quota. improving the living conditions of immigrants in their countries of origin is extremely important, but it is unacceptable to import the problem into europe and to hold all eu countries responsible for the irresponsible behaviour of some member states last year.
in writing. the british have decided to leave the european union. we respect the decision of the british people. but the whole union cannot be allowed to live in uncertainty because of the island. the british must exit the world's largest political and economic alliance quickly and painlessly. in the process of withdrawal, we must act firmly for the interests of the approximately 300 000 hungarians living in europe and on the island. we in the european parliament are fighting the battle: no hungarian citizen should be discriminated against in the uk. if the british want to access the common market in any form, they must accept the free movement of labour. i don't know of any compromise. i can only hope that the hungarian government will also consider the interests of hungarian workers to be paramount. my position is clear. the agreement governing the uk's withdrawal must be subject to the european parliament's opinion. i will never support an agreement that puts english hungarians in an impossible position in london or elsewhere in the uk.
in writing. – europe has a responsibility to help those qualifying for international protection or in need of humanitarian assistance. however, assistance and protection should primarily be granted by the eu in the crisis countries themselves and in their neighbouring areas. there is for example a great need to improve the living conditions of refugee camps in turkey, lebanon, jordan and northern iraq, which offer safe haven to millions of real refugees. i agree with the european people’s party’s position – set out in its political assembly’s resolution of 15 november 2016 entitled ‘protecting lives and controlling migration to europe’ – that there is a need to establish asylum and migration centres in third countries and to create safe zones in africa in which support, assistance and protection could be provided. the possibility to process asylum claims outside the eu should also be an available option. the existence of these facilities outside europe would prevent migrants from embarking on the dangerous journey to europe and thus from being exposed to severe weather conditions in european camps.

in writing. the report on social rights before the european parliament already threatens the sovereignty of the member states and the principle of subsidiarity itself. nevertheless, the document contains many elements that we support and that have already been implemented in hungary. without wishing to list all the achievements, one of the most important achievements of recent times is that 90% of young children receive free meals three times a day and that disadvantaged children receive free meals during school breaks. the minimum wage has also made significant progress over the past 25 years. in terms of institutional placement and long-term care, the report also includes the relocation of disabled people (3500-4000 persons) from large rural institutions to smaller housing units. the reduction in the current rate and the new home-building solutions and initiatives are also important, as from march this year a number of fees and charges related to public administration will be abolished. in order to benefit the poorest consumers, vat has been reduced, inter alia, on milk, eggs and poultry. the digital well-being program also includes a discounted internet package. the action plan for job protection has helped to employ an average of 900 000 workers per year, and has thus significantly increased the employment of women and young people. in the area of tax evasion and avoidance, the success of online collection has increased vat revenues by huf 1000 billion, almost one third compared to 2011.

on behalf of the verts/ale group. – mr president, the energy union is much more than a framework strategy for secure, affordable and climate—friendly energy. sixty years ago, cooperation in the field of energy in europe led to the european union. today, the energy union is a unique opportunity for us europeans to strengthen and reinforce this cooperation in troubled times. for a well—functioning energy union, we clearly need a high level of investment and cooperation among the member states – and not primarily, in terms of gas and electricity, for infrastructure, but also for upscaling renewables and increasing energy efficiency efforts and exploiting the clear synergies between these aspects. the commission again confirms in its report the determination of the commission to make the european union the world number one in renewables, but the legislative proposals of the winter package do not live up to the promises; rather they put brakes on the energy transition and simply do not address key problems and market distortions. there is no real incentive to cut fossil fuel consumption. it allows subsidies to fossil fuel and also keeps the privileges of nuclear energy. loopholes in the energy efficiency directive are left unaddressed as well. according to the report, there was a very modest 0.4% growth in the share of renewables in the eu’s total energy use last year. clearly we must accelerate our efforts. the proposal still does not contain proper incentives for further deployment of renewables. instead it undermines market access for renewable energy sources. while regulated household energy prices in about half of the eu are – rightly – identified as a problem, there are no effective proposals for dealing with energy poverty, instead of those distorting measures. i also see a lack of proper support for prosumers, such as energy cooperatives, to compete fairly in the market. the problem of energy dependency is also well addressed in the report, but the importance of energy efficiency in fighting against it is underestimated. finally, one issue remains almost untouched – the poor enforcement of the existing legislation affecting the energy market. this also implies that the commission must make sure that nuclear installations are not exempted from public procurement provisions or competition law, which currently is not the case. despite the report stating that we are well on track, i believe that a lot more needs to be done in order to achieve a unified energy market and a sustainable, secure energy system. it's a two-card draw. - mr president, i have to go. i would like to assure the honourable member of a quick consensus that i really do think that the steps taken in the field of energy efficiency are very important, if only because they are indeed one of the best ways of combating energy poverty. but i also have a quick question, two. one is that measures and legislation taken here at the european level will probably be adopted by everyone here and then, behind their backs, the governments behind the backs of the members of parliament will whistle on the whole thing. do you see any perspective on how to get governments, member countries, to at least agree on the energy mix? and second, how do you stop governments from making up, upside down, gut-wrenching consumer prices in certain places?
that's a two-card answer. thank you for asking. he asked me about very important matters. on the one hand, the implementation and enforcement of existing energy regulation in the member states is a key issue. and in this i think that the commission is not using all the instruments at its disposal to really implement the provisions relating not only to energy efficiency but also to renewable energy sources, to competition law, to public procurement law in the energy market. the european commission has a task to perform here. the energy union plan and the winter package are quite clear on the need to introduce these price distortion provisions. however, these should be replaced by measures that effectively address energy poverty. this is a primary task for both the member states and the european commission.
in writing. i would like to thank vice president sefcovic for his hard work and persistence. i am particularly interested in the european energy union roadmap which is about to start, where he will visit each member state and hear their views. it is precisely this human and national approach that can make europe strong. the clean energy package opens a new chapter in the integration of the energy union. i think we all agree on the fundamental objectives of the energy union. make europe's energy supply affordable, make markets competitive, while putting the union on a sustainable path. however, the implementation of energy market integration already involves significant risks. these risks can also be found in the clean energy package. we must be careful not to make the same mistake of responding to every change with centralization. in my view, the strength of the european union lies in its diversity. different solutions to different opportunities are driving the continent forward and ensuring renewal. i believe that a better europe does not always mean more europe. the new challenges are not always to be met by the creation of yet another common institution, but by strengthening subsidiarity. i hope that the reform of the price market that is now under way will take account of these principles and that we will find not only a quick but also a suitable solution to the existing problems.
in writing. - mr president, i have to go. the commission's recently published winter energy package is the foundation for the creation of the european energy union and the first building block of hungary's energy independence. by improving energy efficiency, upgrading buildings, deploying renewable energy, we can finally cut hungary off from russian gas pipelines. if energy efficiency increases, not only will the recession bill be cheaper, but tens of thousands of quality jobs could be created in hungary. the deployment of wind and solar power, which are the cheapest forms of energy today, is important in the construction of the energy union. it's hard to understand why they had to be banned in hungary. the energy union also includes the abolition of long-term unfavourable gas contracts with third countries. for example, if we had not renewed the russian gas contract last year, the hungarians would have received their natural gas 40% cheaper today. all because we could buy gas from the world market much cheaper. the energy union would also be good for preventing irresponsible governments from harming their own citizens. the energy union is good for the hungarian people, it gives hungary cheaper and more predictable energy, it leaves more in our wallet at the end of the month. the commission must implement its ambitious plans as soon as possible, only then can we be independent of the russians, only then can we keep the accounts low.
in writing. the vast majority of migrants arriving illegally in europe via the central mediterranean route are economic migrants. the only way to prevent hundreds of thousands of people from risking their lives again by trying to cross the mediterranean to europe once the weather conditions improve is to give special attention to the external aspects of migration. the creation of safe zones outside the eu, the processing of asylum applications outside the eu or at the eu's external borders, and the establishment of migration and asylum centres in third countries are needed. we need to make further agreements to ensure that no new alternative migration routes are created, and stronger action against human traffickers is also needed. it is also important to recognise that rescue at sea cannot lead to automatic legal entry into the eu, as this encourages migrants from north africa to attempt the dangerous crossing in vessels in worse than worse condition in the hope of being rescued. this approach would also be in line with the resolution 'protecting lives and controlling migration to europe' adopted by the general assembly of the european people's party in november 2016, which contains the party family's migration policy. in addition to the central mediterranean region, the western balkan route should continue to receive due attention and support for the countries along this route. it is anticipated that the number of arrivals will increase significantly from march.
in writing. first of all, i would like to congratulate my colleague milan zver on the preparation of this report. this year, more than 3 million students have had the opportunity to study abroad through the 30-year mobility programme. the european parliament will continue to support this great mobility programme so that as many young people as possible can participate and gain not only knowledge but also cultural experiences, experience and new skills. multilingualism is important for improving the labour market opportunities of young people, and therefore more projects are needed under the erasmus+ programme to provide opportunities for language learning and multilingual communication. the main attraction of erasmus+ is that young people gain experience in foreign universities and institutions, enriching them with skills and knowledge that will be a plus for them in the labour market or in starting a business. i think that the programme should also give priority to and support minority language learning. almost all regional and minority language communities face difficulties in ensuring the survival and development of their languages. the erasmus+ programme should support all students, regardless of whether they belong to a majority or national minority community. linguistic mobility not only means that those from a minority community learn the language of those from a majority community, but also the other way around, allowing those from a majority community to develop closer links with minority language communities and to better understand their culture.
in writing. - my fellow members of parliament! erasmus+, now 30 years old, builds on the foundations of european union programmes such as socrates and leonardo da vinci in the 1980s and 1990s. erasmus+ is the largest programme of its kind in european history with the largest budget. so far, more than 3 million students have had the opportunity to study abroad. i am particularly pleased that this is the first occasion on which the eu has supported sporting activities in this way, which i consider to be particularly important for health. the million-strong attendance numbers speak for themselves. studies show that young people who have studied abroad are much more likely to apply for a job later in life. this is very important in the light of the very high levels of youth unemployment in many member states. it is hoped that erasmus+ will continue to be such a success in the years to come, and that many more young people will benefit from their experience abroad, which will be of great benefit to them later on.
in writing. under the current treaty, the union's competences in the field of education and training remain limited to encouraging, encouraging and complementing cooperation between member states, where necessary, and member states have exclusive decision-making power on all matters concerning their national education and training systems. however, the disparities in quality between member states are creating increasing gaps, which the eu cannot continue to ignore. the continuous quality, effectiveness and efficiency of education and training is not only a key element in strengthening economic competitiveness and growth potential, but also one of the most important guarantees for increasing prosperity and preserving and re-establishing social cohesion. education must therefore be a high priority for the european union. the erasmus+ programme should be extended to secondary education and vocational training and the funding for this should be substantially increased.
in writing. sport is a key part of the lives of millions of eu citizens, and the right to sport and physical activity is a fundamental right. the benefits of physical activity are indisputable: it leads to a better quality of life, helps prevent disease, promotes active ageing and has a positive impact on society as a whole, because it transcends ethnic, religious and cultural differences, promotes inclusion, cohesion, integration, and fosters mutual respect and understanding between different cultures. i would like to draw your attention to two important points. first, it is essential to ensure the purity, integrity and transparency of sport in order to preserve its credibility and appeal, especially as more and more money is being spent in the sports sector, in the field of competitive sport, which has become an economic activity. corruption and the use of illicit performance-enhancing drugs are areas that need to be addressed through international efforts and cooperation. the mobility of elite athletes should also be supported in order to enable them to successfully move on at the end of their careers. competitive sports involve long hours of daily training, and the results are the result of daily struggle and sacrifice on the part of the athletes. it is important that when their sporting careers come to an end, they can find a place in other areas of the labour market. their efforts in this direction should therefore be actively supported.

in writing. the success of european climate policy is in the common interest of all of us. just as air pollution does not stop at borders, we cannot tackle this problem alone, we need to come together and find common solutions. but in this fight, we must be cautious at all times to ensure that we achieve a truly successful transformation while protecting the european economy and preserving european jobs. this is the spirit behind the modernisation fund concept, whereby those member states which will be most exposed to additional costs as a result of the new rules will be entitled to a share of the proceeds from the sale of allowances, which they can use to upgrade and modernise their obsolete assets. in my view, the administrative structure for the modernisation fund, as formulated by the envi committee, is opaque and dangerous. a well-prepared system of criteria can adequately enforce common expectations, and an overly complex administrative structure is unnecessary. it is also contrary to the principle of subsidiarity and creates additional tensions to entrust the evaluation of projects to non-participating parties, thereby separating decision-making from implementation. in the spirit of all this, i ask my fellow members to support our amendment to improve the transparency and efficiency of the modernisation fund.
in writing. the debate on the ets directive, which regulates emissions allowances for european industrial installations, has been raging in parliament for 18 months. the reform of the european emissions trading system will include a major element of regulating the sharing of the eu's commitments under the paris agreement, increasing the level of ambition, which can further increase the uptake of low-emission technologies and the transition to a low-carbon economy in europe. however, in addition to ensuring that environmental objectives are met, the legitimate interests of the productive sector must be taken into account throughout the negotiations, as europe may be at a competitive disadvantage by chasing energy-intensive sectors. it is also important to avoid imposing disproportionate burdens on some players in the european industrial sector while giving others undue advantages. i hope that the plenary vote will be able to steer the report in the right direction and to adopt a balanced parliamentary mandate before the trialogue which adequately reflects the above considerations. finally, i think it is important to stress that the part of the environment committee's text relating to the modernisation fund severely restricts the freedom of decision of the beneficiary countries of central and eastern europe. the draft report does not correspond to the political agreement adopted by the european council in its conclusions of october 2014, and therefore i have tabled an individual amendment proposal with my colleagues to ensure that the governance structure of the modernisation fund properly reflects the interests of the beneficiary member states.
the rapporteur. thank you very much for the floor, madam president, and also thank you for the work and cooperation of the shadow rapporteurs. i believe that this time we have succeeded in producing a comprehensive and very substantive report, which complements the european commission's material very well, and which contains a number of proposals and comments to the commission. the report confirms the role of eu competition policy as an essential tool for creating a strong and efficient internal market and as a policy that is also fundamental to economic growth, innovation, resource efficiency and the european social market economy. the future of europe is built on these elements, and ensuring a level playing field is critical to this. the multi-topic report also includes two cross-chapter items. one is the social dimension, as we considered it important to remind member states of the positive impact that strict competition rules that take social aspects into account can have on the lives of european citizens, consumers, workers and businesses. the other is the issue of tax justice. we reaffirm our commitment to tax fairness, which is essential to the integrity of the european single internal market, and we stress that all market players must pay their fair share of tax where those profits are generated. the report recognises the need to put an end to unfair tax competition by member states, as well as to aggressive tax planning, illegal tax optimisation and selective tax incentives that undermine the main sources of innovation, small and medium-sized enterprises. i am pleased that the report has included such an important part of the quest for tax justice, as it is a fundamental condition for a fair competitive environment. the text also calls for tax convergence, which would also significantly strengthen the union's economic cohesion and competitiveness. in addition to tax issues, we are also focusing on the digital single market. the report also underlines the role of smes in the digital single market and therefore welcomes the commission's investigations into the anticompetitive practices of the internet and telecommunications giants. in the context of the digital single market, i considered it important to highlight the issue of sharing economy enterprises. this business model has been in place for a number of years and offers a number of innovative products and services to eu consumers. the commission is not in a position to do so easily when many people want to blend different services into one. and we all know, but just for the sake of argument, uber is not a taxi, and airbnb is not a hotel. they are platforms, but they should be taxed in the same way as the service providers using them. the report also stresses the importance of net neutrality and confirms that, while this is a very important step, the abolition of roaming charges within the eu is not enough: calls within the eu should be regulated at the same level as local calls. this is why our report calls on the commission to come forward with a legislative proposal to regulate intra-eu calls. as regards state aid, the report stresses the increased responsibility of member states towards the commission for aid granted without prior notification. we also stress that temporary state aid in the financial sector was necessary to stabilise the global financial system but must be reduced as soon as possible or fully eliminated and controlled once the banking union is completed. i also think it is important to underline a few other points in the report. corruption in public procurement has a serious market-distorting effect on european competitiveness. in the case of energy and environmental investments, they may be particularly harmful and have a negative impact on the proper functioning of the energy union and the achievement of the union's environmental objectives. accordingly, we urge in the report that all state aid related to energy and environmental investments and all irregularities in public procurement procedures - such as the controversial paksi nuclear power plant expansion project in hungary - should be strictly monitored and thoroughly investigated. the report also addresses the importance of transport infrastructure and addresses the interests of consumers, producers, thus also giving a strong focus to the specific situation of the agri-food sector and paying close attention to enhanced cooperation between national competition authorities. i therefore believe that the text should, as far as possible, give the widest possible indication of the shortcomings in competition policy that are still emerging, both in the member states and in the union. i am convinced that, alongside the recommendations and calls, member states can make a significant contribution to economic growth, the creation of quality jobs, innovation, the development of small and medium-sized enterprises and tax justice.
rapporteur. – madam president, here again i must thank wholeheartedly the commission and commissioner vestager, the shadow rapporteurs and of course those colleagues representing opinions in the various committees, for their contributions. honestly, when we prepared this report it was not a fight, rather it was a spirit of cooperation which prevailed during the negotiations which means, for me at least, that common european policies do indeed have value. but, looking at the practical nature of mankind, i think that competition is never fair. people do compete like this – look at the olympics, or even just a simple football match where the referee needs to intervene hundreds of times during the same match – but the real question is, from the institutional point of view, what we do. this is why i want to congratulate the commissioner once again. she was not discouraged but stepped on such giants as google, uber, bayer, monsanto, apple – i can give you a whole list. this courage is very much needed. on the other hand, i think that what we see now in europe is really a bit frustrating: the big companies, or the bigger ones among them, are practically not paying tax due to various arrangements, but the small ones – sometimes they may die. so this is what we still have to consider. i also very much welcome those comments related to agriculture because here price volatility simply kills. it is not just competition; it kills, it is lethal. so once again, thank you very much for this opportunity, and again my wholehearted thanks to all the colleagues, and let us continue like this.
madam president, i would like to thank dennis de jong for the outstanding work with the report. i would like to welcome the european parliament’s calls for a horizontal instrument for the protection of whistle-blowers yet again and unanimously in the committee on budgetary control and i hope the same for the plenary. i hope the report fits into also the commission’s ongoing work on assessing the possibility for action, as this is clearly a common european interest to have an overall protection for whistle-blowers. horizontal protection is the only way to ensure that all those who report wrongdoings in the interest of the public do not suffer from any sort of retaliation. i believe that it is not only a moral obligation for the eu to act in a context where citizens’ trust in the institutions is low and the perception of corruption is high. it is also indispensable to ensure that eu law is properly implemented in wide areas from the protection of the financial interests of the european union to the environmental acquis. i hope the plenary will vote for this report and the commission is going to act.
madam president, whistleblowers could indeed play a vital role in helping member states and the eu institutions. unfortunately, this is probably only a theoretical possibility, at least in hungary, where the main function of state institutions is to convert public money into private money. government corruption has shaped people's daily lives to the point that people can lose their jobs for responding positively to a post critical of the government on social media. similarly, ngos are under constant pressure, as one criticism of the orbán government has already been labelled as foreign agents. in such an environment, it is very difficult to convince people that reporting abuses is essential to protect the public good and the financial interests of the european union. what a whistleblower in hungary today can expect is the loss of his existence and the squeezing of him in the public service media.

mr president, the central focus of this debate is, or should be: why are we integrating, why these plans to integrate further, what actually is integration for? for us in this house the answer may be self-evident, pro or contra, but for those outside i am far less sure. we assume that european integration is inherently good, on the right side of history, for those who think that history has sides – and i am not one of them. but for many millions in europe the drive to integrate is not self-evident. indeed, they have grave doubts about integration. this debate should also be addressing these citizens by offering a vision that speaks to the great majority, the doubters included. the message, this ideal message, should go back to first principles. european integration becomes legitimate when it fulfils its primary function, that of conflict resolution. anything that generates conflict weakens europe. secondly, the old principle of parity of esteem for all member states, small or large, is inadequately applied. hence the rise in member states’ protection of their national interests. the extent of euroscepticism is precisely the critique of integration that we ignore at our peril.

in writing. paris, brussels, nice, berlin hundreds of innocent civilians have been killed in recent terrorist attacks in these cities. ensuring the safety of european citizens must be the first priority of national governments and the european union. the threat of further terrorist attacks is real. more than 5,000 eu citizens have left europe to take part in the fighting in syria and iraq, and the number of returnees is growing. the highly radicalized, combat-trained returnees are a huge threat. the directive under discussion today is therefore of great importance, since it makes it a criminal offence to attempt to recruit and train, to take part in terrorist training, to travel abroad for the purpose of terrorism, to organise or facilitate such travel, and to finance terrorist offences. in 2016, europol warned that another terrorist attack in europe was likely. the hungarian government is committed to enhancing the security of europe, in addition to protecting citizens, hungary supports all measures that promote the guarantee of the security of european citizens.
in writing. the paris attacks have highlighted a number of shortcomings in the existing european anti-terrorism legislation. the aim of the directive is to eliminate and update the criminal law response to terrorist offences in the european union, to integrate the relevant provisions of new international legal sources, and to ensure a uniform interpretation of terrorist acts in the union. in response to the relatively new phenomenon of 'foreign fighters', the directive also contains new facts, namely attempted recruitment and training, participation in terrorist training, travel abroad for the purpose of terrorism, organising or otherwise facilitating travel abroad for the purpose of terrorism and financing of various terrorist offences. by making these acts punishable, it will be possible to screen out potential perpetrators and thus prevent further terrorist acts. it is welcome that, in addition to the new facts, the directive also focuses on the protection and assistance of victims of terrorism. the hungarian government has supported from the outset any measure that would substantially increase europe's security, including the modernisation of the eu's counter-terrorism framework. ensuring the security of european citizens must be a top priority for all of us, because there is no freedom without security.

in writing. the european union turns 60 this year. brexit does not mean that the eu will retire on its 60th birthday. on the contrary, brexit is a chance for europe to be renewed. keep all that is good and change all that is bad. democracy, the market economy, the foundations of our modern society are under threat today. they're being attacked by populists, nationalists, extremists, who are using the failed ideologies of the past to try to win votes for their short-term goals. these leaders are cutting down the tree for themselves. they could be winners by now. but if it breaks up, europe will be a world apart, we'll be a russian-chinese sphere of influence. with extreme nationalist forces, we can only lose. europe's 60th birthday is not the beginning of retirement, but the opposite: we must issue a new birth certificate for europe. we need to open a new chapter where the solution to problems, to problems, is not to wait until later or to set up another committee to look at problems. the new chapter in europe that is now beginning must focus exclusively on the well-being and security of its people. all our policies should be guided by the idea that people are better off and safer. let's spend the british exit money, 60 billion euros, to overcome the byproduct of globalisation: social inequality. let us use it to make the market economy work for everyone, to stimulate growth and to revive the european dream of 60 years ago. the european dream, where children live better than their parents.
mr. president, while the european asylum system pays special attention to unaccompanied children and minors, the hungarian government would further tighten the laws on refugees. the aim of the new law is to remove unaccompanied children and young people aged between 14 and 18 from the scope of the child protection act. it means that these children are not going to be placed in foster care, they are not going to be assigned guardians, they are not going to be given special protection. the proposal also includes that minors would be automatically detained and would have to wait for the end of the asylum procedure in container camps to be set up in the transit zone. this draft is in no way in line with the un convention on the rights of the child, violates the constitutional obligation of the hungarian state, as well as the principle of protection deriving from humanity. i call the attention of the european parliament and the commission to another inhumane measure by the hungarian government. i believe we have a moral obligation to do everything we can to ensure that this bill never becomes a law.
in writing. among migrants, children on their way to europe are the most vulnerable to harassment and exploitation and the earliest victims of traffickers, so it is important for their own protection that they are registered, thus preventing their disappearance. the most effective way to prevent migrant children from disappearing is to improve the living conditions in camps close to their home countries, so that they do not have to risk their lives and face countless dangers on their way to europe. one of the most important elements of this is ensuring access to proper education, which is essential for the healthy development of children, but also for the future of their own communities - no generations should be left out of education. but it's also important to provide them with proper health care. hungary is therefore also supporting the provision of education in refugee camps in lebanon, jordan and iraq, and is pushing for the construction of a hospital.
in writing. i think the debate on the gender pay gap is important, because today the gender pay gap is around 20% at eu level. it means at least 10 extra years of work for a woman to achieve the same level of pay as a man. the extent of the decades-old pay gap varies across member states, but it is still significant. motivational campaigns have been running for years, such as the 13th annual 'equal pay day'. however, inequalities between men and women are often traced throughout the working life cycle, from job search to retirement. whether women or men are overweight varies by sector, but unfortunately it is common that less than a percentage of women reach leadership positions, regardless of sector. unfortunately, the hungarian government has not done much to end this shameful situation, although it should contribute to it. it should take immediate steps, such as regulating employers to eliminate pay discrimination, or guaranteeing recognition of care institutions and homeworking, for example. i should also stress that the differences in minimum wages in the member states and the lack of a european minimum wage are similar problems to wage discrimination within the eu, and we must implement the latter as soon as possible in line with a common european intention.
in writing. the winters of 2006 and 2009 fundamentally shook the european natural gas system, which had been considered stable. it was previously unthinkable that political battles outside the union could have such a serious impact on our daily lives. while member states were unprepared for the failure of natural gas transport, they managed to survive this dangerous period thanks to joint efforts and solidarity. but these crises entered european history not as threats but as the beginning of a new era. the energy policies of the previously isolated member states began to converge and the issues of security of supply and solidarity were reassessed. we have started to integrate the energy union in a real way, we have started to connect the networks of neighbouring member states and we have made significant steps towards market development. of course, such an integration process should not exclude the often largest-volume interstate contracts. a certain level of information and standard control can improve the bargaining power of member states, thereby contributing to their prosperity and competitiveness. but we must not completely ignore one of the most fundamental laws of the market economy, the right to trade secrets. the union must find the right balance here too: it must focus on a better europe, not more europe.

in writing. the report on the european parliament's budgetary guidelines is the starting document of the 2018 budgetary procedure, setting out parliament's political priorities. the report states that the 2018 process is guided by the motto: 'a union budget for sustainable growth, jobs and security'. hungary has been leading the way for years in sustainable economic growth, increasing employment, reducing unemployment and strengthening the security of its citizens, so this is an important message for us. the report also states that the mid-term review of the current seven-year financial framework should be completed as soon as possible, i.e. the european parliament should contribute to the compromise agreed by the council of member states' representatives as soon as possible. this is key for hungary for two reasons: the council confirmed that reductions in cohesion, agricultural and rural development funds, i.e. funds previously allocated to member states, are excluded from the review. this ensured that the eu funds sought by the orbán government could not be reduced and that the subsidies of hungarian farmers were also ensured. the revision of the mff could also help avoid the liquidity crisis that occurred during the previous seven-year framework, when the commission was unable to pay the invoices submitted to the member states after the projects had been implemented. the council agreement can provide an adequate guarantee: sufficient resources will be available in the annual budgets to pay for the catch-up programmes and agricultural subsidies already in place.
in writing. - my fellow members of parliament! the report on the agenda is not merely a technical document, even if the title might suggest so. the so-called 700 mhz band could be a milestone for the development of ultra-wideband internet and 5g internet connectivity, but also for the development of the entire digital union. the 470-790 mhz frequency band is currently used for digital terrestrial television and for various audio equipment such as wireless microphones. the so-called 800 mhz frequency band (790-862 mhz) is used for broadband services, but this is no longer sufficient for the further development of these services. the essential condition for moving forward is the availability of sufficient radio spectrum. from 2020, the 700 mhz band would also be available for broadband and 5g internet services, thus contributing, inter alia, to improving the quality of digital and broadband services, to full coverage of rural and less developed regions and to ensuring social, societal and digital cohesion in the eu. i would also highlight the objective of concluding cross-border frequency coordination agreements which would allow the future use of the frequency band across borders without interference and, consequently, the gradual abolition of territorial restrictions on digital services. i believe that we should not stand in the way of digital progress and that we should create the right technical preconditions by adopting a report.
in writing. the telecommunications market is undergoing a major transformation. technological progress has made innovative solutions available that have fundamentally transformed the world as we know it, creating a whole new competitive landscape. we don't yet know exactly what technological innovations will have the greatest impact on our future, but if we don't act in time, we are guaranteed to fall behind in global competition. we must not see this transformation as a threat but as an opportunity. if europe can lead the technological revolution, it can put its economy on a growth path that will also give future generations an advantage in international competition. fifth generation telecommunications technology can bring fundamental changes in the field of related services. europe needs to become a market leader in the development and application of these technologies, as competition in the telecommunications market is becoming more intense. the liberalisation of the 700 mhz frequency band is the first major step in this direction. there may be differences of opinion on the timing of the restructuring, but broadband wireless internet access is in the interest of consumers. this will help to reduce the digital geographical gap, improve coverage and increase transmission speeds.
in writing. the european council has finally decided at its last meeting that, due to the opposition of the hungarian government, amongst others, there will be no european public prosecutor's office with competence for all member states, but instead a european public prosecutor's office will be set up with the cooperation of only some member states. hungary, by joining the eu, has undertaken to protect the eu's financial interests in the same way as its own. it hasn't done so, but by withdrawing from government, it's made it clear that it's more interested in committing crimes safely than detecting them. it's a huge profit for the oligarchs around fidesz, the companies that cheat on the vat, because they can continue to practice their state-sponsored practices without any serious controls. although most member states ignore common sense, the problem with the enhanced cooperation prosecutor's office is precisely the same as that of the tax competition: countries that do not cooperate with others create loopholes that allow the system to be circumvented. european vat fraud will then be carried out, to an even greater extent than before, through hungary or poland instead of germany or france. hungary is gradually becoming a tax black hole in the eu. it is essential that in the next long-term eu budget, subsidies should be the limit of the eppo, as this is the only way to ensure the fulfilment of the obligation under the basic treaty. the hungarian government will also submit to the jurisdiction of the eppo if it is the only way to access european funds.
in writing. jean-claude juncker, president of the european commission, recently presented a five-course menu on how to move forward. from the eu's decline into a free trade area, through a two-speed europe, to the image of a progressive federal europe. the uk supports the fifth solution, the creation of a united states of europe. viktor orbán, brexit and other disasters are good for europe to constantly renew itself, deepen its integration. the next stop is the united states of europe, with a common foreign and economic policy, a common army. it's happening right now, right in front of us. we can decide whether to join him. the united states of europe would make us stronger, put us back on the world map, exploit our potential. we would live better and safer in a united europe. with a united europe, we can overcome the byproduct of globalisation: social inequality. we will have the power to make the market economy work for everyone, not just the privileged few, to drive growth. a united europe means unity, not uniformity. it's not a threat to us, quite the opposite. i believe that a united europe is in hungary's best interests.
mr president when we think of the attacks in paris, brussels or berlin, we think first of all of the victims. their senseless deaths force us to consider what we must do to ensure the safety of european citizens. first of all, we must recognize that terrorism cannot be stopped only by fences, by walls, by increasingly stringent laws, by new and new committees. it requires a different way of thinking, a different approach. we have to ask why the descendants of former immigrants who have grown up here in europe are turning against europe, against european values, are becoming radicalised. but we also need to answer how, 70 years after world war ii, neo-nazi groups can flourish in europe and how some of the hopeless working class youth can become violent racists. there is no more important task for us today than stopping radicalization. and that can only be done with a common european programme. a programme that effectively promotes social inclusion, offers a future, a perspective, prosperity, social security for all. if we want to stop radicalization, if we want to prevent more terrorist attacks, we need to create programs that can bring back, win back, those young people who have been pushed to the margins of society and turned to radical ideas. if we can't do that, then there's no point in talking about security and fighting terrorism.
in writing. the events in brussels a year ago shocked us all to the point that such horrors can happen to any of us at any time. they made it clear that we cannot enjoy our freedom without security. these events have made our eu counter-terrorism policy more rigorous and coordinated, but recent events (see germany) are a further warning. the open door policy is being exploited by radicalized groups to infiltrate europe, to abuse the loopholes in the asylum system, the waiting time for applications to be processed. therefore, it is necessary to separate the schengen borders from those who are actually entitled to refugee status, which is what the hungarian government wanted with the new law. according to a europol report last week, there are now more than 5000 international organised crime groups operating in the eu, not a decrease but a significant increase compared to 2013. these international criminal groups are increasingly linked to arms trafficking and migration-related crimes such as human trafficking and forgery of documents. the only way to guarantee the security of our continent and to stop the smuggling of people is to separate economic migrants from genuine refugees outside the european union and to prevent anyone from entering europe unchecked. we need to know who's entering our countries, what their intentions are, and that's the only way to avoid another disaster. we must put the safety of our own citizens first!
on behalf of the ppe group. – madam president, i would particularly like to thank the commissioner for a very thorough exposition of the commission’s position. i think we can all agree on two things: without political parties democracy as we currently understand it is inconceivable. the running of political parties requires ever greater outlays in order to ensure that voters are properly informed of their choices. so if politics is a costly pastime, say, at the member state level, how much more expensive is it at the european level? and as far as this parliament is affected it is this european level that matters. what we are focusing on today is the statute and funding of european political parties and european political foundations. when it comes to funding, the european political parties are currently required to secure 15% of their financing from their own resources. in the light of experience, this 15% should be reduced and the appropriate regulation be amended accordingly. then the definition of a european political party should be looked at again. currently such a party can claim support from parliament on the basis of having one elected mep. this seems too low a threshold and should well be increased. finally, there is the perplexing situation where the european parliament ends up financing parties or foundations that proclaim values entirely at variance with those of the european union and indeed european integration. in a democracy the voicing of such views, repellent or otherwise, can be acceptable but it really is hard to see why the european parliament should be subsidising political forces that are trying to ensure that it is eliminated. so, turkeys voting for an early christmas? well, not exactly, but i can see a certain family resemblance.

on behalf of the verts/ale group. – madam president, let me first of all thank all the rapporteurs for the work they put into this report; considerable innovative energy went into this. sometimes i have the feeling that there is a little too much of this innovative energy. we, in the green group, believe that the report is an important one; the aim of it is a very legitimate and crucial one for the time. had it been about unleashing the full potential of the lisbon treaty in the field of security and defence, we would have been in full support. however, we feel – and i am sorry to say so – that this report is more of a missed opportunity in that regard. (the president cut off the speaker due to a technical problem with interpretation)i am sorry to our italian colleagues for missing the first part. however, the idea that the eda should be transformed into a sui-generis institution and, as such, be the nucleus of a potential european minister of defence is indeed a very controversial one. it would be an agency without transparency, parliamentary oversight, let alone control. we are in full support of centralisation; it makes sense in this field, but not in this way, without any accountability whatsoever. secondly, we believe that, before committing large amounts of fresh money to defence and security, we should explore the full potential of efficiency gains through cooperation, and only after this should we consider additional funding that might be available through the european union budget or from member states. these are the main reasons why our group is unable to support the report, even though we are committed to a stronger european common defence and security policy and there are many very good ideas in the report in general.
madam president, this report could hardly be more timely, and my congratulations to the rapporteur, because for too long europe has lived off the so-called peace dividend and relied on soft power and, in parallel, has tended to neglect hard power. the developments of the last few years tell us why we need to change: the ongoing crisis in the middle east, terrorism, the rule of russia, and more recently the demand by washington that europe must play a significantly more active role in its own defence are the key factors in this respect. yet the possible defence structures of the european union are already in place, notably in article 42 of the treaty. this article is fairly clear in mentioning operational capacity at the european level, common defence, and an undertaking by member states progressively to improve their military capabilities. it could not be clearer. so the scope for change is considerable. to the above, one can usefully add: improvement in security and counterterrorism cooperation – a sensitive area of course. major cost benefits can be expected from joint military procurement. having a shared defence objective can also mean much greater investment in military technology, bringing spill-over with it and the transfer of innovation into the civilian sector. it is clear enough that the private sector is generally risk-averse where blue sky research is concerned, but the state can transform this, precisely because the state can focus on the long term rather than on short-run profitability. there is enormous scope for the european union to push forward in this broad area of common defence.
madam president, my congratulations to mr jáuregui on an excellent report. there is no question in my mind that e-democracy is already happening and will assume ever greater importance in the years to come. various aspects of governance are already structured electronically, so this report is not only timely, but is equally highly cogent. there is an ideal typical model of e—democracy, something along the lines of each and every citizen participating in political decision-making thanks to their computer or smartphone this sounds a little bit like a futuristic version of the classical athenian agora. reality will be different; it usually is. in the first place, not everyone has easy and inexpensive access to the cyber world; there are cost factors that have to be taken into account. second, there are cognitive impediments. some citizens will simply find it difficult to follow the protocols. whether we like it or not, cyber literacy is not universal. equally, the enabling software has to be designed with special care. my own recent experience illustrates this, when i was trying to complete the hungarian interim census form and failing so that i had to deal with a human enumerator. finally, cyber security. the provision of this is a constant struggle, but the technological leap that quantum technology promises in ensuring complete security is not that far away. the eu has to be on top of this, and this report usefully points the way.

in writing. – those who defend the ceu in this debate in the name of university autonomy should be aware of how this autonomy works in practice. obviously, autonomy functions within the law. each member state has its own legal order governing universities. second, accreditation, research assessment, teaching quality measurement are all directed by the state and set often stringent limits to university autonomy. third, the internal administration of universities imposes strict limits on the freedom of what can be taught. the criterion is student numbers. if there aren’t enough, the course will be cut. fourth, research, especially in the natural sciences, is heavily subsidised by the relevant private sector, again a restriction on autonomy. finally, if a university is supported by taxpayers’ money – and almost all are – then that too is a limit to autonomy. after all, the duty to be accountable functions as a restriction. so don’t be carried away by romantic ideas of university autonomy. it has never existed.

court of justice, the european court of auditors, the european ombudsman and the agencies. we had very successful cooperation and sometimes we had long discussions on several issues but, in the end, i believe we were able to find good compromises and good explanations for the questions raised. during the procedure, in addition to examining whether expenditure by the institutions was in line with european regulations, we also concentrated on some specific issues, including transparency, openness of the institutions, access to information, public access to documents in the institutions, and also the whistleblower protection rules of the institutions. the second issue we concentrated on was how aligned spending is with the principle of performance-based budgeting, which was already mentioned beforehand. it is not only compliance with the rules, but also whether the outcome of expenditure is efficient and effective. we believe that the institutions are performing better and better regarding this. we also tried to have a look at the gender balance in the institutions and anti-harassment protection. finally, we believe that it is important to check the emas performance of the institutions and, as my colleague bart staes already mentioned, how the funds are used for co2 offsetting and the fight against climate change. regarding specific institutions, starting with the in some countries. i believe that finally we have some answers. not everything was clarified but i hope that, since i regard the discharge procedure as a long procedure, in the long run we can set up conflicts between the court of auditors and the court of justice as to how the court of auditors obtain access to some information within the court of justice. i am happy that finally – and i hope that we contributed to this procedure – we could find a compromise and the court of auditors and the court of justice agreed on how to settle this problem. i agree with mr staes that the court of on behalf of the verts/ale group. – mr president, dear colleagues, first of all i would like to thank the rapporteur, ms pitera. we had excellent cooperation during the whole discussion period, and i think that finally the outcome of this discussion is really an excellent report, which i recommend to the parliament to support. it is important that in the committee on budgetary control there was a broad consensus on the report. it is a good sign that the different political groups coming with different political backgrounds could agree on the most important issues regarding the protection of the financial interest of the european union. there are several points in the report, and i would like to point out only four important issues from my perspective. first, as was already mentioned, is the question of the european public prosecutor’s office (eppo). i agree that it is an important progress and development that finally we could agree and that as an enhanced cooperation the eppo could start its work within two or three years. on the other hand, we have to keep in mind that this enhanced cooperation at the moment consists of 17 member states, and that some of those member states who are the most critical, from the point of the view of the spending of the eu funds, are not part of the eppo, and this raises the issue: how can we put pressure on those member states where we can experience a high level of corruption, if they are not part of the eppo? from this perspective i believe that the strengthening of olaf competences is something we have to do to have the rights and the competences to better control the spending of eu funds in the member states. the second issue is the anti-corruption report of the european commission. this was published three years ago, and it was promised that every two years we will see an anti-corruption report. now the commission shelved this anti-corruption report: this is exactly the wrong message to send to the member states and those who are in the corruption business if the european commission is not doing its best to mop up corruption in the european union. the third issue is the whistle-blower protection. it was already mentioned that whistle-blowers can contribute a lot to protect the financial interests of the european union. we have to help them and protect them on the european level, not only on the member state level. finally, if the investigative journalism is in line and in parallel with whistle-blowers, investigative journalists can do a lot. thank you for the rapporteur to support this idea. we need to support and protect investigative journalism in the eu.
mr president both the report we are discussing and the debate here in the plenary give the impression to the citizens who are watching us that there are also some very clever crooks in the european union, and that there are governments and authorities in the member states who are trying to fight them, whether they like it or not, and sooner or later they will find a solution. i am missing the special category in the negotiation whereby a government of a member state, by imprisoning authorities operating under the governments of the member states, systematically builds up a system, produces money-spending and taxation practices that are essentially designed to serve the criminal actions of that government. this is the case in hungary, for example, where a barrage of legislation currently ensures that no one outside the immediate family or close circle of friends of prime minister viktor orbán can access the benefits of various development projects funded by the european cohesion funds, for example. obviously these governments - because i think there are more of them - are also opposed to the creation of the european public prosecutor's office, because they would rather, in order to protect their own criminal, corrupt interests, by taking advantage of the legal problems of other member states, stay out of this special form of european cooperation. i ask the commission to pay attention to the context in which member state governments unfortunately capture authorities and thus deliberately cause damage by committing crimes to the financial interests of the european union.
in writing. the european parliament's committee on budgetary control prepares its annual report on the fight against fraud in parallel with the closure of the accounts. the report shall outline the efforts made to protect the financial interests of the community. it calls on the member states to strengthen cooperation and control in the detection of fraud cases, which are essential for more effective action. one of the biggest problems remains the smuggling of high-tax goods, which causes a serious loss of revenue for the eu and member states' budgets. customs and border controls at the external borders of the union need to be strengthened in order to combat smuggling and fraud more effectively. the report also shows that statistics based on the number of cases of fraud and irregularities reported by member states can often be distorting and that it is therefore essential to distinguish clearly between errors and fraud. stresses the role of the european anti-fraud office in protecting the financial interests of the european union, but regrets that the average duration of investigations carried out by olaf continues to increase. i supported the report in committee and in the plenary vote.
madam president, please. my fellow citizens! in the context of the questions put to the commission, i would like to point out that it is necessary to assess how the technological and innovation environment will affect the recognition of qualifications. let us not forget that, for all its faults, the european union is one of the most balanced regions in the world. why is that important? the integration of migrants from outside the eu, often without even a basic qualification, into the labour market may take many years, even a decade, according to un data if we consider the lack of language skills. to give you an example, a technician in a north african country was able to learn his trade on machines that represented technology that was decades old. the qualifications of this person cannot be practically equated with those of a european professional. as technology advances at a faster pace, these questions are becoming more and more real. we can't make a political issue out of this. i therefore support in particular the amendments which protect the high quality of qualifications. this is a fundamental interest. we need to strengthen the european union's competitiveness in the future.
in writing. the eqf is a common european reference framework linking the qualifications systems of different countries. this framework acts as a kind of translation tool, making qualifications acquired in different countries and education and training systems in europe more readily and mutually understandable. it has two main objectives: to promote lifelong learning and to promote mobility between citizens and between countries. experience so far shows that member states are not sufficiently committed and, despite their commitments, there is still a lack of transparency of qualifications and a low recognition rate of foreign qualifications. to that end, the eqf requires continuous development and adjustment, and should be supported and improved by regular monitoring, in particular as regards its user-friendliness, interoperability and transparency. i therefore support the member states' commitment to implement and use the programme effectively in order to ensure its success. the effective functioning of the edf will help sectors and individuals to make the most of the opportunities offered by the internationalisation of qualifications; it will also have an impact on education and training systems, the labour market, industry, trade and citizens. the european union should make it possible for everyone, regardless of age or status, to make their skills and competences, including those acquired through voluntary activity, more visible and to promote them in a clear and accessible way, especially in cross-border areas.
mr president there have been several different figures, several thousand million tonnes, over the course of this debate, but let's face it, the food wasted in europe is expressed in euros, and experts estimate that it amounts to 140 billion euros a year that we throw away in the form of food. this is about the same size as the annual budget of the european union itself, so it's an insane amount. but there is a phenomenon i would like to draw your attention to, which is in hungary, my country, where, despite social decline, food waste is increasing. and the reason for that is probably that poor people can only afford to buy food that is very perishable, perishable food. i wanted to draw attention to the fact that social status also plays a role in food waste.
in writing. - mr president, i have to go. the figures for food waste are staggering, especially when you consider how many millions of people are malnourished or starving today. i believe that the european union, as one of the richest and most prosperous societies in the world, has a moral and political responsibility to reduce the enormous amount of food that is thrown away every year. in view of all this, the report of the committee on the environment is very important and timely. one of the key objectives of the decision adopted today is to facilitate the work of civil and religious organisations involved in food donation, as the donation of undelivered food throughout the supply chain not only significantly reduces food waste but also helps those in need. i am very pleased that the plenary report contains my amendment to encourage the use of the funds provided by the european union's food aid programme, the fead, to deliver surplus food. in many regions, particularly those in disadvantaged situations, the biggest challenge is to get existing foodstuffs to those in need and to set up and operate the appropriate logistics. it is also necessary to promote good practice in the member states to exclude the liability of the donor for food donated in good faith and fit for consumption at the time of donation, taking full account of food safety rules.

madam president, officer, i have to go. hungary has from the outset taken the position that relocation cannot be compulsory and has therefore challenged this at the court of justice of the european union. at the same time, 23 member states voted today for a mandatory quota. yet, in a year and a half, only 18,000 migrants out of 160,000 were relocated. this demonstrates the inadequacy of mandatory quotas and confirms that any allocation can only be voluntary. the immigrant resettlement program without a cap on numbers currently under discussion and the penalty that is in the offing are also unacceptable. this is not the only way to put the principle of solidarity between member states into practice. solidarity can also be expressed through border protection, police cooperation and humanitarian programmes. hungary has so far spent some eur 900 million on border protection and the hungarian government is continuously expanding its humanitarian engagement in the middle east. mandatory resettlement is seen as an invitation to migrants and an incentive to smugglers. so as long as we allow hundreds of thousands of people to enter europe unchecked, as long as we do not separate economic migrants from genuine refugees outside the borders of the union, any future mechanism based on mandatory migration quotas or similar will be counterproductive and unsuitable to address the problem.
