in writing. syria is no longer a symbol of the peaceful coexistence of different religions, although it was for centuries. i recently had the pleasure of hearing the story of father jacques mourad, who served for 15 years as prior of the 5th-century monastery of al-qaryatayn in syria. this place has been a model of peaceful coexistence between christian and muslim communities for centuries. each year they remembered together st. elias, the patron saint of the monastery, who would rather die a martyr's death than deny his faith. it's all in the past. after 15 years of church and community building, father jacques mourad was kidnapped by isis fighters, the monastery was ransacked, and his holy tomb was looted. the priest was held by isis for four months, a small bathroom served as his cell. he was beaten daily with the most varied psychic terror, but he never gave up his faith, his smile. in his darkest hours, he had a vision that said, "you're coming to the light". this light shines before him now, after his release, as he personally takes the news of the syrian christian communities, whose lives are in danger on a daily basis, to the world. i thought this story was important because it shows that the threat to christians in the middle east is not an old legend, not part of ancient history, but it is happening right now, right before our eyes. four out of every five victims of religious persecution worldwide are christians. we must use every tool at our disposal to protect the christian communities in the middle east.

in writing. we are living in difficult times: the eu's competitiveness is declining, unemployment is still high, youth unemployment is also causing huge tensions and the migration crisis remains on the agenda. in this period, the term 'social dumping' is increasingly used in the arguments of both the european commission and some western member states, whereas the mobility of european citizens and businesses should not be reduced, but on the contrary, increased. it is important to take into account that the eu's global competitiveness has been declining since the 1990s. what is needed is not a multi-speed europe but improved competitiveness and a truly single market where capital and goods, services and labour can move freely. so we need more transport links, more trade, and more better, cheaper services to keep consumers spending more and creating more jobs. in a multi-speed eu, regional development policy (the continuous upgrading and convergence of objectives) would also be called into question, even though its balancing function was one of the main attractions of accession, as was the single, free internal market. the european commission should therefore, rather than reducing mobility, act against any member state measure restricting the free movement of services in the field of road freight transport.

in writing. winning consumer confidence is key to the single market. this is closely linked to the fact that consumers cannot be misled by differences in the quality of the basic components of the same products. the harmonization carried out between member states is aimed precisely at overcoming such obstacles and it is therefore unacceptable that the basic ingredients of the same products show significant differences in quality between the different countries or regions of the union. the explanation that some products, such as coffee, are adapted to the taste of the users is still acceptable. however, the fact that the use of the same brand and packaging of detergents produces clean, fragrant clothes in one country and a lack of efficiency in another is difficult to explain by the different needs of consumers. the fact that second-class goods of inferior quality are being sold at the same or even higher prices on the shelves of the markets of central and eastern european countries is unacceptable and contrary not only to consumer protection rules but also to the principle of the single european internal market, a phenomenon that has been going on for decades. i do not think that the solution is to create a 'nutella police force', but consumers rightly expect the commission to investigate these cases thoroughly and impartially in order to put an end to this phenomenon.

madam president, europe is ashamed that more than 70 years after the unimaginable tragedy of the holocaust, anti-semitism is still alive and well and is not only alive but is growing in many member states. that is why we have a moral duty, not just a political one, to do everything possible to combat anti-semitism and to ensure the safety of our fellow jews. it is totally unacceptable that in europe today, anyone is threatened because of their jewish identity or religion. it's disgusting that politicians who claim to be moderate use veiled or sometimes not so veiled anti-semitic clichés, sometimes even here in the european parliament. i support proposing that the ihra definition of anti-semitism be widely used. without it, it is difficult to monitor, to follow, to document the spread of anti-semitism, its manifestations in europe. this definition does not in any way restrict freedom of expression. and perhaps most importantly, it makes it clear that insulting the state of israel is also a form of anti-semitism, because it attacks israel for being a jewish state. at the same time, any criticism of the policies of the current israeli government is not anti-semitic. we must stand not with any israeli government, but with the state of israel and the jewish people. we are bound by european tradition and by the memory of the millions of victims of the holocaust.
in writing. it sounds cliché, but in europe it is a very real, dramatic experience: mass violence and murder begin with words, and the propaganda of hatred and discrimination that poisons social consciousness can lead to tragedies of historic proportions. the increasing prevalence of anti-semitic and racist rhetoric and atrocities in europe requires urgent action by the union and all european democrats. this requires a clear language, a definition of anti-semitism that is interpreted in the same way in public discourse and applied consistently in legal practice, and i am convinced that this decision will prove to be an important contribution to stopping this dangerous process over time. for this reason, the resolution stresses the primary responsibility of political leaders, governments, in combating anti-semitism. the only reason is that there are still european politicians who are not prepared to address voters with extreme racist tendencies by means of their open or coded anti-semitic expressions. a month ago in this very room, at our previous plenary in brussels, viktor orbán did the same thing when he blamed his government for all its troubles on the american billionaire speculator george soros. in eastern and central europe this evokes serious memories, and we understand the anti-semitism that is hidden in such references. according to an interview, this is exactly what vice-president timmermans meant, but his position at the time did not allow him to react to orbán's outrageous remark. but we as meps will not tolerate anti-semitic rhetoric here or anywhere in europe from politicians in charge.
mr. president, i have to ask you... i understand that the idea of unilateral concessions is treated with some reservations by some of my colleagues who fear for the position of european agricultural producers. however, i would point out that this measure has both a principle, a policy and a practical significance, and that it is worth considering them in their interaction. as a representative, i myself am particularly sensitive to the protection of the interests of european agriculture, but in this case the list of ukrainian export products covered by the facilitation was preceded by a genuine impact assessment and can be further strengthened. the draft legislation already provides adequate guarantees to prevent possible market theft, since the commission may intervene at any time in a specific case, either on its own initiative or on the initiative of a member state. in this situation, the european union must be ready and able to express our solidarity with a free, european ukraine in tangible forms, including in this case trade concessions.
in writing. the free trade association with the european union has given ukraine a new opportunity for development. customs liberalisation can expand the current trade relationship between the european union and ukraine. this will open new doors for attracting capital to ukraine and for foreign entrepreneurs, and will open up opportunities for ukraine to cooperate with the european union and its neighbouring regions, and to implement joint cross-border projects. this will create new jobs and economic and infrastructural developments that will improve the living standards of the population. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the union's macro-financial assistance to ukraine in order to ensure that the union's macro-financial assistance is consistent with the union's macro-financial assistance. the opening of trade with the european union requires, first of all, improvements in border infrastructure and logistical and transport conditions. unfortunately, the reforms implemented so far in the country are causing a number of social and economic tensions. it is therefore essential that trade reforms and the economic development of the country take place in parallel. the implementation of the market economy in ukraine and its further integration into the union internal market should be an important objective. economic subsidies, reforms can only have their maximum effect by completely eradicating corruption.

in writing. - mr president, i have to go. this june, the united nations will host a high-level conference to advance global ocean governance, which will help to implement sustainable development goal 14. oceans and seas play a central role in our lives and are key to our well-being and future. we know that at the johannesburg summit, the world committed to reducing overfishing by 2015. 31.4% of the world's fish stocks are still overfished. this not only seriously threatens some marine ecosystems, but also poses a serious threat to food security and the economic and social sustainability of coastal communities. in particular, illegal, unreported and unregulated fishing poses a serious threat to fish stocks. the eu under the leadership of the european commission should take a comprehensive approach and convince partner countries to accept that we can effectively tackle illegal fishing. please support the draft ep report which has been tabled and which could point the participants in the un ocean conference in the right direction.

in writing. - mr president! as a member of the committee on regional development and as a supporter of the opinion drawn up by regi on this subject, i would like to draw attention to the regional aspects of the subject. territorial and social cohesion should also be taken into account in the development of new legislation and in the implementation processes. the digital backlog in less developed rural areas is very high compared to urban areas. statistics show that some regions have a broadband coverage of 28%, which is almost 50% below the average coverage. the benefits of broadband and 5g technology could bridge this digital divide. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the union's policy on the environment and its implementation in the member states. this requires the creation of dedicated financial funds that encourage investment in less developed regions. in addition to territorial cohesion, we must also pay attention to digital cohesion, to ensure a level playing field for european citizens wherever they live in the union.
in writing. - mr president! the events of the past few months have shown us that europe can no longer afford to follow world events with a blind eye and look to the outside for solutions to its problems. we have to take our destiny into our own hands, and we have no choice but to be at the forefront of solving global challenges, so that the world aligns with our solutions. the fourth industrial revolution is happening right before our eyes. new technologies, digitalization, are impacting every level of the economic model we've been used to, and they have the potential to fundamentally reshape the existing wealth relationship. our role in this transformation will define europe's competitiveness and economic strength for decades to come. with a well-founded strategy, we can put the union's economy on a new and stronger footing and return to the forefront of the world. however, such a major transformation presents both opportunities and threats. as the structure of the economy changes, jobs are not only created, they can also be lost. with the right strategy, we can manage this and even expand the workforce, but we have to be able to identify the fundamental forces that are driving the transformation. in hungary, we believe that the development and expansion of technical and it training is key in this situation, as europe currently needs about 180 thousand more it specialists. i think the hungarian example can be exemplary.
mr president, villages across europe, which currently offer very limited opportunities compared to urban life, are threatened by depopulation. this process is already causing serious cultural, social and economic problems, which are only intensifying over time. especially when there are governments in some member states, such as hungary, which seem not to hesitate to steal even the millions received from the union for food packages. the revitalisation of european villages is therefore one of the major challenges of the coming period. i would like to refer to the initiative of the european parliament and the european commission 'smart villages', which aims to revitalise european villages through substantial improvements in the living conditions of rural populations and focuses in particular on young people. internet access, quality education, advanced health care, good transportation and of course access to meaningful jobs. at present, these services are the privilege of the urban population. with a smart approach, we can make our villages not only more livable, but also more innovative.
madam president, following this debate, i see that in all european countries there are very serious problems with regard to freedom of the media. and perhaps it's worth mentioning the lessons for a moment: if and when the european institutions, the guardians of the european values, the european commission, had stepped in at the right time, for example in hungary, when the government controlled the media almost 100%, we might not be here. because if we had an example of the strength of the european union's institutions, we wouldn't have to face our own weaknesses.
mr president, the treaties establishing the european union set out the objective of promoting the development of our peoples, stressing that economic integration must also help raise living standards. the most important tool for this is cohesion policy. the report shows that after 2008, disparities between member states and regions have increased sharply, with social and economic inequalities becoming more acute. the differences are most pronounced in wages. this is the main way in which the people of the countries that joined 13 years ago judge the success or failure of the so-called union project. the wage gap within the union is not good for the developed countries either. millions of people in less developed countries are forced to work in developed countries to earn a living, causing low wages and social tensions. recognising this, jobbik for hungary launched the european citizens' initiative, which aims to raise wages in europe, finding partners in seven countries. the european commission welcomed this initiative, acknowledging its legitimacy and committing itself to initiating legal acts once one million signatures are collected. one important area for this could be a new cohesion policy that supports human resource development and companies that produce high added value, and hence the growth of wages itself.
in writing. cohesion policy is one of the eu's most important policies and an expression of european solidarity. catching up with poorer member states improves the eu's position in global competition, and strengthening social cohesion is a prerequisite for continued european cooperation. however, the implementation of cohesion policy needs to be improved in several respects: some member states receive a sustained amount of cohesion funds equivalent to 2 to 3% of their national income without achieving any progress in catching up with the old member states or reducing territorial and social disparities. these problems seriously threaten the future of cohesion policy. in any event, when planning cohesion policy beyond 2020, these causes should be identified and addressed. according to many studies, one of the most important reasons is elite-driven corruption, which also influences the selection of development goals, and which can only be curbed by a thorough reform of management and control mechanisms and by increasing the proportion of national participation in programmes. the creation of a european public prosecutor's office (eppo) to prosecute crimes against the eu's financial interests will contribute to the fight against corruption involving eu funds in the member states participating in the establishment of the eppo. however, the eppo does not include a number of countries that are serious contributors to the funds. the commission has also received a number of complaints from the member states, including from the polish authorities, the european parliament, the council of the european union, the european parliament, the european economic and social committee and the european parliament.
in writing. - mr president! the affronte report also acknowledges that the level of overfished fishery resources in the mediterranean is two or three times higher than the maximum sustainable yield. the mediterranean, the cradle of european culture, is threatened with becoming a desert, despite being one of the most biodiverse areas in the world with its 17 000 species. we must also recognise that, in addition to overfishing, the mediterranean is threatened by a number of other factors, such as pollution, changes in coastlines and habitats, and oil and gas exploitation. it is clear from the above that we must gradually give way to environmentally friendly and modernised marine and freshwater fisheries, in addition to fishing. for example, a significant proportion of the salt lakes in hungary are located in natura 2000 areas. these fish farms do not represent any threat to the natural values of these areas. in fact, in many cases it is our closed systems of wetlands that have created and preserved unique associations of aquatic habitats. the future european fisheries policy will therefore have to devote much more eu resources to environmentally friendly aquaculture and fisheries than at present.
mr president, a recent analysis suggests that some 15 000 jobs in the transport sector in hungary could be threatened by restrictions imposed by some western european member states. the last such measure is the restriction introduced by germany on 25 may, which states that the 45-hour rest period cannot be spent in the cab of the vehicle. however, the infrastructure was not in place, as there were not enough protected parking and accommodation spaces to meet the increased demand. the cargo, the vehicle and the fuel are all at risk, but the vehicle left unattended could be used for a potential terrorist attack, and the question of responsibility is not clear. without the creation of preconditions, this does not contribute to safety and improves the conditions of drivers, but gives the impression of a mere protectionist measure. i therefore ask the member states concerned to review their measures and not to penalise foreign carriers until they have created the preconditions for compliance with the provision.

in writing. the effort sharing regulation, which addresses greenhouse gas emissions outside the eu emissions trading system, is one of the key tools for the european union to meet its paris commitment. in my view, the reduction targets set out in the legislative proposal are sufficiently ambitious and adequately reflect the fair contribution of each member state to the common emission reduction ambitions. at the same time, i think it is important to emphasise that for some countries the target reductions could be better aligned with their pre-2020 emission capabilities. a target setting that is more responsive to early reductions in the pre-2020 scheme would therefore be acceptable, as well-performing member states cannot compensate for these reductions without the possibility of transferring allowances, while less well-performing member states can easily achieve the desired targets by 2020 by transferring essentially worthless allowances due to oversupply. it is also important to ensure that the targets reflect the actual level of development of each member state.

mr president europe today seems to be in a state of confusion, facing many unprecedented challenges and conflicts. today i think that europe, the future of all of us, is at stake. we must see that europe has reached a turning point. the end of a europe based on more or less homogeneous nation states, industrial capitalism, well-organised social classes. the new industrial revolution, the fragmentation of social classes, cultural diversity, globalization has created a new world in which the old answers, the old habits, are no longer helpful. we have to redesign, we have to re-create europe. i believe that the new europe must be built on progress and solidarity, on freedom and security. we must build a europe that has no room for hatred, stigma and discrimination. we want a europe that is welcoming and open, a europe where everyone can find a home, where the future is not fear but hope and opportunity.
mr president in her introduction, mrs veld spoke of the parties she called nationalist, claiming that for them there was a voice, a truth. aren't you the ones who think that there's only one way to europe? the deeper integration? and those who want looser cooperation, you label them as anti-european, and that's the most subtle sign? in his message, manfred weber, leader of the pp, referred to the christian roots as the basis of the value system. on the other hand, it says that we need diversity here. you have quoted the founding fathers so many times, and i will now quote two statements by robert schuman: 'europe cannot be integrated by creating a common structure, which you want, and the other is that europe will either be christian or it will not be.' that's what the founding fathers said, you're just picking and choosing between their statements.

in writing. mr president, at the beginning of 2006, the spring european council committed itself to taking the necessary measures to reduce child poverty rapidly and substantially, ensuring equal opportunities for all children, regardless of their social background. yet, to this day, one in four children in the eu is at risk of poverty. this is a problem that must be addressed seriously, and it is only through specific programmes that we can hope to find a solution. whether it is local initiatives based on social cohesion, such as every child lives well, or public programmes. i think hungary is leading the way in this area. school meals are now available not only during the school year but also during all four school holidays, so that children from disadvantaged families can have access to hot meals during the summer holidays. since 2015, 208 000 disadvantaged and overburdened children across the country have been provided with one free hot meal a day. i think it is important that these important and exemplary programmes should be discussed here in the european parliament.
in writing. hungary has an interest in supporting and strengthening families. hungary has been following its own path and has been spending more and more (more than 4,5% of gdp in 2018) on family support, which is almost twice the oecd average. according to surveys based on the eurostat methodology, the proportion of people at risk of poverty or social exclusion has shown an improving trend in poland since 2012. the largest proportion of these improvements were among children under 18, more than 40 000. in hungary, not only the most disadvantaged children but all disadvantaged children are important in the fight against child poverty and they receive support through the child welfare programmes. the number of 'safety start' orphanages where children and parents receive services together in disadvantaged communities is already well over 100 nationwide (the number has more than doubled since 2012). hungary is also at the forefront of compulsory kindergarten attendance, with children starting kindergarten at the age of 3 at a rate almost twice the eu average. since 2010, the hungarian state has increased its spending on childcare by 250%, and already during the school (inter) holidays, it provides one hot meal a day for disadvantaged children. a specifically targeted eu resource in three counties (szabolcs-szatmár-bereg, hajdú-bihar and borsod-abaúj-zemplén) is used to provide a food package to children under three years of age, helping some 12 000 disadvantaged children.
madam president, we can start from the basic proposition that there does indeed exist a european level of politics: the institutions of the european union, the member states and the citizens interact in a variety of ways to make this european political system operative. let me make it clear that, in many ways, this political system is quite different from what is to be found in a member state. then there is the question of european—level political parties and their role in the system, and, in this connection, how these parties should be financed. this financing raises a number of issues. first of all, do they exist, these parties? well, obviously, the answer is yes, they do. secondly, should they exist? well, if we have european—level elections for the european parliament – as we do – then logic dictates the answer: yes. thirdly, if these european political parties exist, then can they function in any way that makes sense without adequate finance? again, logic says that they need financing. at the same time, the system as it exists – and others have referred to this – can be gamed. virtual parties, cross—party memberships, ad—hoc groups, etc., have been known to gain access to financing. the regulation should ensure that this practice comes to an end. both these issues should be resolved in the regulation, and resolved speedily.
in writing. there are some hot policy topics that are talked about a lot. my impression is that the european industrial strategy is not one of them. but there's a lot at stake here: jobs, knowledge and tradition. there is no doubt that the key to the success of european industry is quality, excellent value for money. therefore, two pillars are worth focusing on: innovation and competitive price. first, appropriate regulation should promote the growth of r & d expenditure in a technology-neutral way. secondly, we must give all the support we can to the industry to enable them to sell their products at competitive prices on the global market. for energy-intensive industries, the level of energy prices is of decisive importance. with the creation of the single energy market, we have started to reduce energy prices. however, there are still some pending energy policy issues that may have a negative impact on market developments. a prime example is the authorisation of the nord stream gas pipeline extension, which could contribute to higher energy prices in some member states. i would like to draw the commission's attention to the fact that the above aspects - innovation and competitive energy prices - should be given the highest priority in the further development of the industrial strategy.

in writing. i would like to thank my colleagues elmar brok and silvia costa for the work they have done in developing the future eu strategy for international cultural relations. throughout history, the importance of using cultural diplomacy tools to shape a country's international image, promoting international relations with so-called soft, i.e. cultural means, has been proven many times. i appreciate the recognition of the importance of cultural diplomacy in external relations and the prominent importance of culture in today's globalized, accelerated, depersonalized and christian values-subverting world. the european union must be a unified representative of european values, with human dignity, freedom and equality at its core. a balanced approach to strengthening cultural links that respects cultural differences is therefore important. it is also appropriate for the european union to use cultural instruments in shaping its foreign policy strategy, and this can be particularly effective in developing relations with third countries. the report focuses on strengthening cultural links with countries in the eu's neighbourhood policy. the so-called cultural visa programme is designed to facilitate the exchange of experience between artists and to strengthen cooperation between cultural institutions in eu member states and third countries. i am pleased that the report encourages the creation of programmes by which the eu facilitates the participation of third countries in crossborder cultural and scientific projects, which also serve this purpose.
member of the commission. – madam president, it is pleasure to be in the parliament today together with my colleague, commissioner oettinger. yes, to achieve the consensus on how we are going to fight against fraud against the union’s financial interests by means of criminal law, this is a historical moment. i am happy to be here at this historical moment in spite of the late hour. i would particularly like to thank the two co—rapporteurs, ms gräßle and mr lópez aguilar. without your tireless efforts and your continuous support in moving this initiative forward, we would not be here today. a quarter of a century after the adoption of the convention on the protection of the european communities’ financial interests, it was time to modernise the legislative framework on the protection of the eu budget. the directive updates the definitions of criminal offences affecting the union’s financial interests. it also introduces common rules as regards sanctions and statute of limitations linked to these offences. the anti—fraud directive is a decisive step forward and, in combination with the european public prosecutor’s office, we are entering a new era. the anti-fraud directive will essentially frame the activities of the european public prosecutor’s office (eppo). it will provide the material scope for the investigations and prosecutions of criminal offences falling within its competence, and will ensure that the eppo can rely on harmonised definitions of these offences across the member states. the council reached a general approach on the eppo in enhanced cooperation in june, and this chamber will soon be asked for consent. i am confident that the eppo regulation can be adopted as soon as possible with a strong backing from parliament. i am happy that 20 member states have already notified their participation in the eppo. this will make it an efficient tool to protect the financial interests of the eu, and i invite more member states to join. the work has just started. it has not finished with the adoption in the council. i will finish now because i know that we are in a hurry here. i will now be happy to hear my colleague, mr oettinger.

in writing. by 2020, wifi4eu will support the establishment of free wifi hotspots in local communities (in public spaces, parks, hospitals, libraries and other public places) across the union. the wifi4eu initiative is a highly forward-looking initiative, as free wireless internet access in public areas benefits all citizens. the union will provide eur 120 million to implement the initiative, which will finance the equipment needed for public wifi services in 6-8000 municipalities. public authorities (local authorities, associations of local authorities) can apply for the programme if they wish to provide free wifi access in public areas not covered by such service, either public or private. i am pleased that the negotiations between the eu institutions have managed to strengthen the geographical balance between member states in terms of access to resources. the wifi4eu initiative is in line with the digital wellbeing programme's goal of making the internet accessible to all, and i therefore support the idea of as many hungarian public administrations as possible applying for the programme.

in writing. states that recognise the importance of spreading digital technology in all areas of life are successful. and not just in the capitals, but in the suburbs. that's why it hurts that according to the latest measurements, in 2017, one fifth of hungarians have never used the internet. the situation is even worse if we look at rural areas only: three out of 10 people in the low countries and the south of the danube do not have an internet connection. and only a third of the poorest hungarians have access to the internet. that means 2.5 million people are not getting a chance to participate in the digital world. the spread of the internet and digital technologies is not an end in itself, which ends with more computers or larger monitors in homes, in schools. digital tools enable the expansion of opportunities, the expansion of job opportunities, lifelong learning and the creation of equal opportunities. if you have the internet, it's easier to find a job, work from home, learn a foreign language. in the 21st century, the internet is a fundamental right, essential for participation in the information society, for eliminating inequalities, for personal development. that is why we have long advocated that the fundamental right of access to the internet be guaranteed at eu level.
madam president, the regulation on the security of gas supply clarifies several important and useful technical cooperation issues. at the same time, it is clear that the basis for security is whether there is enough energy and whether there are enough alternating transport routes. it is quite clear that the european union cannot give up russian gas, so safety would be represented by alternative routes, and it is therefore incomprehensible that the european union has thwarted south stream due to different competition rules, while allowing north stream 2, which is not an alternative. the current regulation infringes the principle of subsidiarity in several respects, the regions to be created in the event of supply disruptions would be established by the commission on a delegated basis, and the european commission also has a practical veto on cooperation with third countries, which infringes the autonomy and interests of the member states.
madam president, mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, let me first of all thank mr buzek for his work. he had a lot to do, because the first proposal that the commission put on the table was unfortunately bleeding from several wounds. examples include the ill-considered list of regions, the double-counting of reporting obligations and the weak definition of solidarity actions. the report shows significant improvement on all these points. instead of pre-established regional groups, regions are developed along the lines of risks. the compromise figure reached on the reporting threshold dispels any appearance of a difference between the old and new member states. for solidarity actions, the description of the conditions under which and how member states are to assist each other is much more detailed. i believe that guaranteeing the security of gas supply in europe is a matter that we must constantly deal with. with more and more experience, the revision of the security of supply regulation will therefore be a permanent task. however, it is safe to say that this report will further strengthen the regional cooperation and solidarity that is so badly needed in the energy sector, especially in the countries of east-central europe.
mr. president, we are discussing a very, very important european regulation today. this debate here in the european parliament is about how we can provide cheap and safe gas to people in europe. natural gas prices in europe have fallen by almost half in recent years, but hungarian people are still paying the same price as they were three years ago. the difference between the two prices was transferred to swiss offshore accounts. fidesz, under the banner of regime lies, is charging every hungarian family a surcharge of 23,000 forints for gas every year. if it weren't for this corrupt pricing in hungary, every hungarian family could save a month's heating bill every year. but the reality is that you can't do that in hungary. fidesz businessmen and their russian friends made 50 billion forints in extra profits last year alone because of corrupt gas pricing. it is unacceptable that gas prices in hungary are much higher than they should be due to such unfavourable gas contracts. if we really joined the european energy union, we wouldn't have to turn off the gas in the homes of a hundred thousand hungarians every year. that's why we choose europe and the european energy union over orban's corrupt gas pricing.
mr president, the well-known egg scandal has highlighted the need not only to improve the alarm system but also the feeding technology in poultry farming, the need for european action on the withdrawal period for chemicals in the food industry and the lack of animal welfare support for poultry. the solution can only be at european level. it is time to support the common market with an even stronger common food and feed safety system. in the event of an egg-scale crisis, a multiannual crisis fund system may be the solution, rather than the current practice of annual crisis funds.
mr president, i voted in favour of this report because providing free wi-fi is a big step towards closing the digital divide and creating a digital europe. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the new rules and to monitor the implementation of the new rules. i am particularly pleased that this has been targeted by the smart villages initiative. in addition to full internet coverage, it can be used to achieve further improvements in european villages. the 'green walls' initiative will create a living, active digital solution environment that will stop the destruction of villages, displacement and rural decline. the 'wifi4eu' programme is therefore an excellent fit for the idea of smart villages, and could be the first step towards a digital europe for many municipalities.
mr. president, ladies and gentlemen, on the occasion of the signing of the istanbul convention, i would like to inform you that as a criminal lawyer and as a member of the women's rights committee, i have embarked on an important research project, the conclusions and results of which i would like to share with you. the essence of my research is this: i want to look at closed criminal cases where the victim died as a result of domestic violence, partner abuse or harassment, and i want to identify common factors in the case history. i want to establish how effective early intervention could have prevented these tragic deaths. i think it's very important that we recognize that domestic violence, partner abuse, harassment are potentially life-threatening acts, so we need to finally rise above the various ideological battles that are now gender, not gender, this party, that party, there is no place for party politics, which is why i ask everyone to come to the research report regardless of party affiliation. i'm calling you with love and i'm waiting for you.
mr president, i voted strongly in favour of the report because i agree with the commission's proposal that the eu should join the istanbul convention. it is the first comprehensive, legally binding instrument to prevent and eliminate violence against women and gender-based violence. unfortunately, hungary has not yet ratified this convention either, as its government does not put the issue on the agenda in the hungarian parliament. it seems that the hungarian government wants to hide behind the european union's skirt on this issue, that is, they want to exempt themselves from national ratification, while domestic violence kills more victims than terrorism. so let there be zero tolerance for violence against women. let this be the first step on that road. in fact, i would go even further: zero tolerance for violence against women, children and the elderly.

in writing. in her opening speech, frederica mogherini high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy reported on the increase in the number of returns of illegal migrants, the strengthening of the crackdown on human traffickers and the assistance provided to the libyan coastguard. i agree with all of these measures, but they alone cannot solve the current migration crisis. there is only one solution, and that is to separate economic migrants from genuine refugees before they enter europe. this approach was outlined by the hungarian prime minister more than a year and a half ago in his ten-point action plan, known as schengen 2.0. it must be made crystal clear that only genuine refugees and only legal ones can enter the territory of the european union. at the same time, the eu must finally acknowledge that any mandatory migrant quota scheme is not a solution to the migration crisis, as it is seen as an invitation to illegal migrants and an incentive to smugglers. even the member states supporting and accepting the migrant quota have not implemented the resettlement of the migrants sent to them, so it is possible that only 25% of the target number could be relocated a few days before the decision expired on 26 september. this highlights once again the fact that the migration quota approach is not fully suited to tackling the problem. it's not about bringing trouble, it's about bringing help.

in writing. i have repeatedly stated in my work in parliament that the european union must pursue a consistent policy towards the western balkans if it is to preserve the commitment of the applicant countries to european integration and values. the candidate countries of the western balkans need a clear message, and i therefore welcome the words of president juncker in his state of the union address on enlargement policy. i am pleased that after some previously contradictory statements, it has been made clear that the european union is ready to accept new member states in the future, that the number of eu member states is not limited to 27. this political message should have been made earlier, but better late than never. now that it has become clear that the political will is there, i would once again call on the european commission and the governments of the western balkan countries to seize this opportunity and work towards speeding up and deepening accession negotiations. thank you for listening.
in writing. the most important question today is the future of the union: whether the bloc of 27 member states will stay together or become two-speed, with those who are left behind pushed to the periphery. in his speech, president juncker committed himself to preserving the unity of the eu, a major step forward from last year's white paper on the possibilities of a european union. this means that he recognised that a multi-speed europe would cause serious damage not only to those who were left behind, but also to the 'core of europe': the former can suffer not only from the draining of eu funds, from the absence of policies that benefit them, and from the lack of a unified foreign policy. the risks for the hungarian countries are to allow the creation of a 'periphery' with political, economic and social uncertainties, thereby exposing the eu to the influence of russia, among others. it is therefore important that president juncker's sixth plan addresses not only pan-european problems but also those of the countries of the southern and eastern periphery: social problems, helping those outside the euro area, food quality, proving the commission's sensitivity to problems affecting the union as a whole. equally important is the president's warning: helping is important, but eu rules must be respected by all. it's not an option, it's a duty. president juncker's plan is welcome, but the devil is in the details. as legislators, we must therefore ensure that the 'europe of equals' is truly achieved with the participation of all member states.
in writing. the european union has faced significant, often dramatic challenges in the past year. there were many who had already envisioned the end of the union, its disintegration, its collapse. today, however, the union is beginning to overcome its difficulties. this is true even if there is little progress in the brexit negotiations and the number of refugees has not decreased significantly. the greatest threat to the eu today, however, is not these events but rather the illiberal democracy represented by the hungarian and polish governments, which is fundamentally attacking the eu, the values on which the eu is built. it is unacceptable that some member states refuse to accept the judgment of the european court of justice. the danger is not only that this ideology, based on primitive images of enemies, is easily marketable, but also that the eu has no real tools against these member states. the commission's role in the fight against terrorism is to ensure that the european union is able to take effective action against terrorism. a two- or multi-speed europe is not a real solution. the consequence of this is that the countries with anti-european governments will be left on their own. i am convinced that europe today needs not more division but much more unity.
in writing. it is time for european integration to move beyond the paralysis of recent years marked by the economic and financial crisis, the refugee crisis, brexit and other developments that have strengthened disintegration. i therefore welcome the fact that the president of the commission's report has set out initiatives for the coming period which could help to make the union more dynamic, more efficient and more transparent in a number of important areas. our urgent tasks to strengthen our international competitiveness; to defend our internal and external security together and more effectively; to have a new, functional and humane common migration policy; to reform the community budget; and, last but not least, to finally make real progress in the implementation of social europe require that the eu once again functions as a genuine community of solidarity. the selfish, nationalistic policies and the undermining of the principle and practice of solidarity by some governments in recent years should, in my view, be addressed not by two- or multi-speed integration schemes but by a shared and full responsibility for collective decisions reflecting the interests of the community. integration and the future of european citizens may also require the enforcement of democratic rule of law and solidarity. in response to the lack of solidarity, political will and action on the part of the governments of the member states, too often manifested at council level, we look forward to further, innovative and forward-looking initiatives that put the direct interests of the population first, for which i am convinced that the commission can count on a strong ally in the majority of the ep, including the european socialists.

in writing. erasmus is undoubtedly one of the eu's most successful mobility programmes, having so far enabled more than 3 million students to study abroad and provided a total of 9 million young europeans with the opportunity to gain experience abroad. the european parliament remains committed to the programme so that as many young people as possible can participate and gain cultural experiences, experience, new skills and abilities beyond knowledge. the programme has undoubtedly had a positive impact on the professional and personal lives of young people not only in europe but also in neighbouring and candidate countries. the results so far show that almost 90% of all participants in erasmus vet programmes have seen their employment increase as a result of the experience. however, in the face of the continuing high unemployment rate, erasmus+ should strongly support actions to create better employment opportunities. it should be emphasised that the budget of the programme will need to be increased in the next budget period (2020-2027) and additional resources will be needed to ensure that as many young people, students and teachers as possible have the opportunity to participate in the programme.
in writing. the european parliament's report on the new european skills development has many elements that support the education policy of the orbán government. the development of e-skills, the modernisation of vocational training systems and the introduction of dual training have all been given high priority in our country since 2010. the hungarian government began to develop and implement comprehensive national strategies for digital skills and developments in e-skills years ago. currently, huf 270 billion of eu aid is available for the period 2014-2020, of which only huf 125 billion is spent by the government on infrastructure development. this means, first and foremost, new schools and the renovation of existing ones with a radically renewed educational toolbox. the remaining part will be used for content development, such as the development of digital curricula and the new national curriculum (nat). the commission will also be investing substantial amounts in the digital development of schools outside the central region, the number of mobile devices in schools will be increased and 50 000 teachers will be able to participate in further training on the use of new content and tools. however, the left-wing factions submitted an amendment at the end, which vaguely refers to the requirements for migrants, which can be interpreted in several ways, including support for illegal immigrants, which the hungarian people's party faction unanimously opposed. in view of all this, i abstained at the end of the vote on the adoption of the entire document, since a good professional report was essentially overwritten politically and irresponsibly.

in writing. the proposal to be put to the vote aims to transpose into union law the conservation, control and enforcement measures adopted by the international commission for the conservation of atlantic tunas in the atlantic ocean and adjacent seas. should we support the mandatory nature of recommendations, since by protecting marine fish stocks and preserving marine biodiversity and ecosystems we are in fact ensuring the future and livelihoods of our children and grandchildren, many of whom will hopefully be involved in marine fisheries? in this context, it is important to note that, unfortunately, aquaculture is currently secondary to fisheries in the eu. however, it is clear that sustainable marine fisheries cannot meet the growing demand for fishery products, and the european union must shift towards aquaculture development through a qualitative shift. this will increase the competitiveness of the sector and reduce europe's dependence on imports of fishery products. the eu produces less than 2% of the world's aquaculture fish, but it is a world leader in quality. this quality must be the basis for further work, thus helping the growth of the sector. this not only means more jobs, but also contributes to improving food security.
in writing. digitalisation and the spread of the internet are changing our lives at a rapid pace. new tools and new connections are opening up new horizons and creating efficiencies that were previously unimaginable. we're spending more and more time online, and we're seeing more and more connected devices in our lives. while these make life quicker and more convenient, we should not forget that such rapid change is fraught with danger. the anonymity and encryption that comes with the internet is a great cover. in recent years, conflicts have included armed conflict and war in cyberspace. now cyber warfare is on par with conventional weapons and every major country has its own hacker squad. however, cyberattacks are not limited to conflicts between states, but also to the exploitation of the internet by many independent and anonymous organisations, as evidenced by the data theft and ransomware attacks of recent years. in an environment of such uncertainty and threats, our priority is to protect the security of european citizens. the protection of services, infrastructure, property and personal data is as important as physical security. i believe that cybersecurity is one of the most pressing issues of our time and requires rapid and effective cooperation.

madam president, anyone following the relationship between the european union and the united kingdom since the brexit vote will be struck by a number of factors. first of all, why is everything in slow motion? the brexit referendum took place well over a year ago, yet the pace of discussions has been glacial. second, the united kingdom never seems to have believed that the eu 27 would preserve its unity. indeed, the uk made a serious diplomatic effort to split that unity. this failed. third, the eu’s position was made very clear indeed in the council mandate and in the various statements by michel barnier. despite the clarity, the message never seems to get through. the uk side, when it responds, does so in vague all too often unfocused language or makes complaints of inflexibility or punishment. fourth, the discussion in the uk itself seems to be entirely unaware of what the eu is saying. it is as if the uk has become another planet. from the outset, in the 1970s – and i was there – the uk never sought to understand what the eu was for. the attitude was entirely transactional. equally england – and i mean england, and not the uk as a whole – has become intensely focused on itself, thereby making it very hard to see the eu as it is, a body of values.
in writing. the brexit negotiations have been going on for more than six months, but there is still no real progress. the british prime minister's speech in florence has given some momentum to the negotiations, but there are still significant differences of opinion. this is particularly true for the approximately 3 million eu citizens living in the uk. there are growing signs that the uk authorities are already discriminating against eu citizens. that is why i support the union's proposal that eu citizens living in the uk and a collective of british citizens living in the eu should be given the same rights. this includes family reunification and the maintenance of previously acquired rights. this solution would also significantly improve the situation of hungarians living and working in the united kingdom. after all, the orbán government does nothing for these people, it has left them alone. it not only restricts their voting rights, but it also does not care about their social security. but for us, the cause of our fellow citizens in the united kingdom is an important, defining european issue. we will only vote on the outcome of the brexit negotiations if it clearly protects civil rights and ensures the rights of our fellow citizens that we have already won.

in writing. it will be particularly important this november to make concrete, substantial progress at the bonn meeting in order to maintain the momentum of the paris climate agreement in 2018 and beyond. in addition to the practical implementation of the agreement, one of our biggest challenges is to maintain the level of ambition and to keep the issue of climate change on the agenda. the negotiations also face the challenge of defining common characteristics of national commitments, while at the global level, the proposed management of losses and damage by developing countries is a conundrum for europe and developed countries. a growing number of citizens are taking action to decarbonise in order to become more resilient to climate change. i particularly welcome the commitment of non-state actors, such as religious organisations active in the protection of creation, to the paris agreement. it is important to harness this potential and to align it with commitments at national level. accordingly, we should assure them of our recognition and support. i believe that sound national commitments can only be defined with the involvement of the non-public and private sectors, which requires encouraging dialogue between public and non-public actors.
mr president, an airline’s operational measures cannot be influenced but, in every circumstance, airlines must act in accordance with the high standards of the consumer protection rules established by the eu. therefore the failure to inform passengers or others of passenger entitlements, as happened in the case of ryanair, is unacceptable. after announcing thousands of flight cancellations, ryanair failed to inform passengers on several occasions that they have an option to be re-routed with other airlines where there is no suitable ryanair service available within a reasonable time frame. this kind of misinformation has negative consequences beyond the damage done in the given case. when consumers are misled in such a way it discourages them from seeking access to their rights and destroys the enormous work that has been done on empowering eu consumers. ryanair must meet its legal obligations and act in compliance with eu rules, which means it must take responsibility for the re-routing of passengers, including with other airlines. however, we must not forget about the damage that has been done in the field of consumer rights awareness.
in writing. there has been a lot of talk lately about the fact that the wealthy airline ryanair has had to cancel thousands of flights, affecting the lives of tens of thousands of passengers. a few days later, the company announced an even bigger wave of cancellations during the winter schedule. the reason for this was to improve the punctuality of their flights, which had fallen from 90% to 80% in the first two weeks of september, and to switch to a new holiday system that would bring the holidays forward to the calendar year instead of april-march. the negative impact of these measures is the high number of flight cancellations. what the company must bear in mind is the european regulation 261/2004, which strictly regulates the rights of passengers in case of flight cancellations. it is very important that ryanair takes this into account and acts accordingly in the case of injured passengers. it is not enough to compensate those affected, but it must also provide, where appropriate, hotel accommodation and other services to its stranded passengers in accordance with the provisions of the regulation. and yet, there are thousands of people who may be in a situation where they've already booked their accommodation, bought their museum tickets, bought their concert tickets for specific dates. in such cases, it is important that the parties concerned are individually assessed. i can only hope that the company learns from this situation and acts with much more caution in similar cases in the future, always keeping in mind the rights and interests of passengers.

member of the commission. – madam president, thank you very much for this very interesting and important debate. i will react to what we have just heard. this is eu money and it requires a response from the eu institution. i think that is very clear. in the eu we see that crime is on the rise, cross-border crime is on the rise, we see permanent structures working against our interests, against our taxpayers’ financial interests, and we need to come with a permanent structure. this is a very practical response we are coming with. this is a very important moment for me too, because i worked for many years in cohesion policy, and i always found this to be a policy which is driven by a very strong ethical impetus. there is solidarity in it and there is a very strong, legitimate expectation from the european taxpayers that we will guarantee the protection of their money; and we have to do that! this is what we are doing now by the european public prosecutor’s office (eppo) which is not replacing national prosecution services. it is going to work in a very strong synergy with national prosecutors, which will come into play in cases where the national prosecutors cannot come with an efficient response. here we heard a lot about the missing, non-participating countries. three countries – the uk, ireland and denmark – have a permanent opt-out from criminal justice policies. what about the remaining five countries? i had to convince many more than those five, where we have failed for the time being. this was a very important time when, yes, we had yellow cards and it forced me to go to the national parliaments and have very intensive communications with the national authorities, national co-decision-makers in the field of the fight against crime, arguing that this is a very important step forward. i had to bring a lot of arguments, facts, figures. you mentioned eur 500 million. i will add eur 50 billion which we have as an estimation of the cross-border carousel vat fraud. that is why it is very important that we added this cross-border carousel fraud under the remit of the eppo. this was a real game-changer, and i must again thank those who worked on the fifth directive when we added this to the remit of the eppo. we had to solve the unsolvable problem: we had the duty or the task not to touch the national criminal prosecution, because the eppo will work under national criminal prosecution rules; and i am sure we have managed to do that. so, what about the missing member states? i will keep trying to convince them. from sweden and the netherlands i heard the arguments: our prosecution works well. i have to be better in explaining that this is not about well or badly functioning prosecution services, it is about guaranteeing the protection of your taxpayers’ money. so this is my task for the near future. on poland and hungary i have never hidden that i see the need to take the eppo as a factor in the negotiations on the future multiannual financial framework and for the possible future cohesion policy. i will be very strong on this because i am convinced that the eppo will work as a very strong deterrent and, let me use the word, repressive body. if a member state does not have the eppo then we will have to be much stronger in preventive action. already now in the cohesion policy, in structural funds distribution, there is a lot of control, a lot of audit, a lot of preventive measures, and still you see so much fraud and corruption in it. for the member states which will get the cohesion fund money and will not be under the eppo we will simply have to be much stronger in enhancing this control and audit. this will be my effort for the months to come when we discuss the future cohesion policy. thank you very much for your support. i hope the vote tomorrow will reflect the mainly positive debate here in parliament, and i will keep you informed about the next phase – after, i hope, having the final adoption by the council – about the practical steps towards the implementation of the regulation and full functioning of the eppo.
in writing. i agree in principle with the objectives behind the establishment of the european public prosecutor's office (eppo). however, respect for the principles of subsidiarity and efficiency must be a matter of paramount importance, which is why hungary has decided, along with a number of other member states (such as the netherlands, sweden, malta, poland, denmark, ireland and the united kingdom), not to participate in enhanced cooperation. the establishment of the european public prosecutor's office - as an independent institution - raises the question of its compatibility with the fundamental law in my country, since according to the fundamental law the hungarian public prosecutor's office is the exclusive enforcer of the state's criminal claim. there are several eu institutions, such as eurojust or olaf, which deal with the perpetrators of crimes against the eu's financial interests, and these should be strengthened rather than creating a new eu institution.
in writing. the european public prosecutor's office is the first eu body to be able to protect the financial interests of the union through the means of criminal law. this is a major breakthrough, even if not all member states are joining the eu-wide law enforcement organisation. the eppo is responsible for the prosecution of all criminal offences in the country. but that is not true. the existence of the eppo does indeed affect the sovereignty of the member states, but in the area of criminal offences that are not currently prosecuted at all or not effectively by the member state authorities. for example, the circle of cross-border vat fraud. the commission has also received comments from the complainant, the complainant's representative and the complainant's representative. but we can't afford to be inconsistent. i therefore call on the commission and the council, and on my colleagues, to allow the benefit of the eu's financial penalty system only if the powers of the eppo are accepted when the next long-term financial framework is adopted. in the absence of this, the criminal law system becomes untenable: financial crimes, including vat fraud, will then be committed not in germany or france, but in hungary or poland. and if we make eppo membership a precondition for spending eu money, we can trust that even the most corrupt governments will be more willing to comply, because they cannot afford to lose their funds.
in writing. the proposal to set up a european public prosecutor's office is one of those that life has really forced to protect the interests of european taxpayers in a concrete and effective way. i therefore urge the ep's strong agreement and swift implementation. unfortunately, experience over the past few years has shown that the judicial bodies in the member states are not equipped to detect and punish abuses and fraud committed in the use of european funds, and in some cases they are not reliable. the treaty does not provide for such authorisations for olaf, eurojust and europol, as we know, because the legislator simply could not have foreseen a situation where complete corrupt mafia systems could be established within the union with government support, preying on european funds. it is a sad fact that the most obvious example of this today is my own country, the eppo's - not accidentally - non-cooperative hungary, where orbán's authoritarian, anti-european regime is based on systematic corruption, funded in large part by the embezzlement of european funds, surrounded by internal ad-hoc legislation. this practice is covered up - and not covered up as it should be - by the prosecutor's office, which is under the regime's tight political control, where olaf's inquiries are routinely blocked. i am confident that in the foreseeable future all member states, including my own country, will be part of the important cooperation that is taking place within the european public prosecutor's office.

mr president, health, food safety and sustainable food production are all important aspects in relation to fertilisers. we have to state that these substances are crucial for agricultural production, but, as in many cases, their overuse can be dangerous. therefore, the precautionary principle should be first, while mitigation can be realised by smart precision techniques. i welcome the harmonisation steps between inorganic and organic fertilisers, and also the steps in the field of cadmium levels. regarding the latter topic, i think that the proposed commission timeline could be more ambitious, and i would be glad to introduce even lower levels in the eu. there are already existing low cadmium levels, for example in the case of hungary, where the limit is set at 20%, and everybody is happy. i can imagine that gradually the levels can be reduced. because of all these aspects, i am going to support the good results achieved at committee level and also the more ambitious socialist amendments.
in writing. the completion of the common market in plant protection products will improve the competitiveness of european agriculture and will enable a common minimum health level to be established in the union. however, stimulating the economy cannot be the sole objective. it is not only the workers directly affected by the regulation that need to be considered, but also all those indirectly affected by the issue. in this case, it's a very significant group: every single european citizen who eats food produced here every day. the most controversial point in the report is the cadmium levels in phosphate fertilisers. cadmium is a toxic substance to the human body that accumulates in the human body and, above a certain level, causes serious damage and cancer. it is in the common interest of all of us to keep cadmium pollution from fertilisers below critical levels, even if this step leads to a deterioration in the competitiveness of polluting plants. hungarian regulations are strict and progressive on cadmium. the gradual reduction of cadmium levels proposed by the european commission and agreed by the european parliament at committee level requires adjustment to the current hungarian level. this is the right direction and i cannot support a proposal which multiplies the carcinogens in the food consumed by the hungarian population.
in writing. the world of drugs is a rapidly changing world that increasingly threatens the physical and mental health of our children and young people. drug dealers are now present in the vicinity of our schools, where young people themselves have increasing access to these drugs. the fight against drugs can only be tackled in an integrated way: prevention, rehabilitation and law enforcement are also essential. every effort should be made to ensure that young people, teachers and parents are properly informed. it is also essential that synthetic substances, which are perhaps the most toxic, be placed on the list of prohibited drugs as soon as possible. all the time lost is measured in human lives in this area.
in writing. the hungarian minimum wage is a disappointing figure in european comparisons. almost everywhere in europe, the minimum wage is higher than in hungary. slovaks, croats and poles earn tens of thousands of forints more. the germans get three times as much, but even the slovenes get twice as much as the hungarians. the situation is further aggravated by the fact that 4 million people in hungary live in poverty, and almost 7 million people live overnight with zero savings. a german or a danish car mechanic makes three times as much as a hungarian for the same job. therefore, a european union minimum wage should be established, which would allow us to increase the wages of hungarian workers by leaps and bounds. if there is a guaranteed european minimum income at the eu level, we can dramatically improve the living standards of hungarians, they should no longer worry about whether they will get out by the end of the month. at the same time, the minimum income could also ensure that equal pay for equal work is guaranteed. the work of a slovak or a pole is no more valuable than that of a hungarian.
